But in effect all unhappiness and lamentation were immediately dissipated, and people became joyous. Jagannāth Miśra, the father of Gaurahari, was completely overwhelmed with happiness.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="108">
<h5>108</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>āchāryaratna, śrīnivāsa, jagannātha-miśra-pāśa,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;āsi’ tā̐re kore sāvadhāna</strong><br>
<strong>korāilo jātakarma,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;je āchilo vidhi-dharma,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tobe miśra kore nānā dāna</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Chandraśekhar Āchārya and Śrīvas Ṭhākur both came to the house of Jagannāth Miśra. They performed the different ceremonies according to religious principles which are prescribed for the birth of a child. Jagannāth Miśra also gave away much wealth in charity.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="109">
<h5>109</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>jautuka pāilo jata,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ghore vā āchilo kata,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sob dhon vipre dilo dāna</strong><br>
<strong>jata nartaka, gāyan,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhāṭa, akiñchana jon,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dhon diyā koilo sabāra māna</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Whatever riches Jagannāth Miśra had collected in the form of gifts and presents, and whatever he had in his house, he distributed among the <i>brāhmaṇs</i>, singers, dancers, poets and the poor. He honored them all by giving them riches in charity.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="110">
<h5>110</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrīvāsera brāhmaṇī,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nāma tā̐ra ‘mālinī’,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;āchāryaratnera patnī-soṅge</strong><br>
<strong>sindūra, haridrā, toilo,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;khoi, kalā, nānā phol,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;diyā pūje nārīgoṇa roṅge</strong></p>
<p class="translation">The wife of Śrīvas Ṭhākur, whose name was Mālinī, accompanied by the wife of Chandraśekhar and other ladies, came there in great happiness to worship the baby with such articles as vermilion, turmeric, scented oil, a kind of puffed rice called <i>khoi</i>, bananas and coconuts.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="111">
<h5>111</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>advaita-āchārya-bhārjyā,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;jagat-pūjitā ārjyā,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nāma tā̐ra ‘sītā ṭhākurāṇī’</strong><br>
<strong>āchārjyera ājñā pāyā,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gelo upahāra loyā,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dekhite bālaka-śiromaṇi</strong></p>
<p class="translation">One day shortly after Gaurahari was born, Advaita Āchārya’s wife, Sītā Ṭhākurāṇī, who is worshipable by the whole world, at the request of her husband, went to see that topmost child with all kinds of gifts.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="112">
<h5>112</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>suvarṇera kaḍi-bauli,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;rajatamudrā-pāśuli,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;suvarṇera aṅgada, koṅkoṇ</strong><br>
<strong>du-bāhute divya śaṅkha,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;rajatera malabaṅka,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;svarṇa-mudrāra nānā hāragoṇ</strong></p>
<p class="translation">She brought different kinds of presents made from gold, such as bracelets, coins, necklaces, ankle ornaments, a conchshell covered with gold, and other kinds of jewellery for the child.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="113">
<h5>113</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>vyāghra-nakha hema-joḍi,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kaṭi-paṭṭasūtra-ḍorī</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;hasta-padera jata ābharaṇ</strong><br>
<strong>chitra-varṇa paṭṭa-sāḍī,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;buni photo paṭṭapāḍī,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;svarṇa-raupya-mudrā bahu-dhon</strong></p>
<p class="translation">She also brought a locket with a tiger nail set in gold, belts made of silk and lace, silk saris and shawls, as well as silk clothing for the newly born child.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="114">
<h5>114</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>durvā, dhānya, gorochana,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;haridrā, kuṅkuma, chandana,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;moṅgol-dravya pātra bhoriyā</strong><br>
<strong>vastra-gupta dolā chaḍi’&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;soṅge loyā dāsī cheḍī,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;vastrālaṅkāra peṭāri bhoriyā</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Riding in a palanquin covered with cloth and accompanied by maidservants, Sītā Ṭhākurāṇī came to the house of Jagannāth Miśra, bringing with her many auspi - cious articles such as <i>durvā</i> grass, rice paddy, turmeric, kumkum and sandalwood. All these presentations filled a large basket.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="115">
<h5>115</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>bhakṣya, bhojya, upahāra, soṅge loilo bahu bhāra,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;śachī gṛhe hoilo upanīto</strong><br>
<strong>dekhiyā bālaka-ṭhām,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sākṣāt gokula-kān,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;varṇa-mātra dekhi viparīto</strong></p>
<p class="translation">When Sītā Ṭhākurāṇī, bringing with her many kinds of food, clothes and other gifts, came to the house of Śachī Devī (the mother of Gaurahari), Sītā Ṭhākurāṇī was astonished to see the newly born child, and she could appreciate that except for a difference in color, the child was directly Lord Krishna of Gokul, Himself.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="116">
<h5>116</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>sarva aṅga—sunirmāṇa, suvarṇa-pratimā-bhāna,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sarva aṅga—sulakṣaṇamoy</strong><br>
<strong>bālakera divya jyoti,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dekhi’ pāilo bahu prīti,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;vātsalyete dravilo hṛdoy</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Seeing the divine effulgence of the child, the well formed different parts of His golden body which were covered with auspicious signs, Sītā Ṭhākurāṇī was very much pleased, and because of maternal affection, she felt as if her heart were melting.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="117">
<h5>117</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>durvā, dhānya, dilo śirṣe,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;koilo bahu āśīṣe,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;chirajīvī hao dui bhāi</strong><br>
<strong>ḍākinī-śā̐khinī hoite,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;śaṅkā upajilo chite,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ḍore nāma thuilo ‘nimāi’</strong></p>
<p class="translation">She blessed the newly born child by placing fresh <i>durva</i> grass and paddy on His head and saying, “May you live long.” To protect Him from ghosts and witches, she gave the child the name Nimāi.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="118">
<h5>118</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>putramātā-snānadine,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dilo vastra vibhūṣaṇe,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;putra-saha miśrere sammāni’</strong><br>
<strong>śachī-miśrera pūjā loyā,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;monete hariṣa hoyā,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ghore āilā sitā ṭhākurāṇī</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Sītā Ṭhākurāṇī gave gifts to the mother and child, at the time of His birth and when the child was brought from the residence where He was born back to the home of Jagannāth Miśra. Then Sītā Ṭhākurāṇī, being honoured by mother Śachī Devī and Jagannāth Miśra, returned to her own home in Shantipur.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="119">
<h5>119</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>aiche śachī-jagannāth,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;putra pāyā lakṣmīnāth,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;pūrṇa hoilo sakala vāñchito</strong><br>
<strong>dhon-dhānye bhore ghora,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;lokamānya kalevara,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dine dine hoy ānandito</strong></p>
<p class="translation">In this way, Śachī Devī and Jagannāth Miśra, having obtained a son who was the husband of the goddess of fortune, had all their desires fulfilled. Their house was always filled with riches and grains. As they looked upon the beloved body of the Lord, day after day their happiness increased.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="120">
<h5>120</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>miśra—vaiṣṇava, śānta, alampaṭa, śuddha, dānta,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dhon-bhoge nāhi abhimāna</strong><br>
<strong>putrera prabhāve jata,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dhon āsi’ mile, tata,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;viṣṇu-prīte dvije deno dāna</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Jagannāth Miśra was an ideal Vaiṣṇava, a devotee of the Lord. He was peaceful, restrained in the enjoyment of his senses, pure and self-controlled. He therefore had no desire to enjoy material opulence. Whatever money that came because of the influence of his divine son, he gave in charity to the <i>brāhmaṇs</i> and for the worship of Lord Viṣṇu.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="121">
<h5>121</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>lagna goṇi’ harṣamati,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nīlāmbara chakravartī,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gupte kichu kohilo miśrere</strong><br>
<strong>mahāpuruṣera chihna,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;lagne aṅge bhinna bhinna,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dekhi, — ei tāribe saṁsāre</strong></p>
<p class="translation">After calculating the astrological chart for the child, Gaurahari, Nīlāmbar Chakravartī privately said to Jagannāth Miśra that he saw all the different auspicious symptoms of a great personality in both the body and horoscope of the child. He revealed that in the future this child would deliver the whole universe.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="122">
<h5>122</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>aiche prabhu śachī-ghore,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kṛpāy koilo avatāre</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;jei ihā karaye śravaṇa</strong><br>
<strong>gaura-prabhu dayāmoy,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tā̐re hoyena sadoy,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sei pāya tā̐hāra charaṇa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">In this way the Lord, out of His causeless mercy, made His advent in the house of Śachī Devī. The Lord, Gaurahari, is very merciful to anyone who hears this narration of His birth, and gives that person the shelter of His lotus feet.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="123">
<h5>123</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>pāiyā mānuṣa janma,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;je nā śune gaura-guṇa,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;heno janma tāra vyartha hoilo</strong><br>
<strong>pāiyā amṛtadhunī,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;piye viṣa-garta-pāni</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;janmiyā se kene nāhi moilo</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Anyone who attains a human birth but does not have the opportunity to hear of Gaurahari is unfortunate. Instead of drinking nectar from the river of devotional service, if one drinks from the poisoned well of mundane happiness, it would have been better to not have lived at all.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="124">
<h5>124</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-chaitanya-nityānanda, āchārya advaitachandra,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;svarūpa-rūpa-raghunātha-dāsa</strong><br>
<strong>ihā-sabāra śrī-charaṇa,&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;śire vandi nija-dhon,</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;janma-līlā gāilo kṛṣṇadāsa</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Taking on my head as my own wealth the lotus feet of Śrī Chaitanya Mahāprabhu, Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu, Āchārya Advaitachandra, Śrī Swarūp Dāmodar, Śrī Rūpa and Śrī Raghunāth and all the devotees of Mahāprabhu, I, Krishnadās, sing the glories of the Lord’s appearance.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-prabhu-kohe-kohilan-ei-mahamantra" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="">
<h2>Prabhu kohe <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h4>by Śrīla Vṛndāvan Dās Ṭhākur</h4>
<p class="verse"><strong>hare kṛṣṇa hare kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa hare hare</strong><br>
<strong>hare rāma hare rāma rāma rāma hare hare</strong><br>
<strong></strong><br>
<strong>“prabhu kohe—kohilaṅ ei mahāmantra</strong><br>
<strong>ihā japa giyā sabe koriyā nirbandha</strong><br>
<strong>ihā hoite sarva-siddhi hoibe sabāra</strong><br>
<strong>sarva-kṣaṇa bolo, ithe vidhi nāhi āra”</strong><br>
<strong>“ki śayane ki bhojane, ki vā jāgaraṇe</strong><br>
<strong>aharniśa chinta kṛṣṇa, boloho vadane”</strong><br>
<strong>“āma-prati sneha jadi thāke sabākāra</strong><br>
<strong>kṛṣṇa vinā keha kichu nā bolibe āra”</strong></p>
<p class="translation">“The Lord said, ‘I have told you this Mahāmantra, so everyone now chant it in proper measure. By doing this, you will attain success in all respects. There is no other rule but to chant it at every moment.’ Whether resting, eating, or waking—day and night think of Krishna and call His Name aloud.”<br>
<i>(Śrī Chaitanya-bhāgavat Madhya 23.77-78, 28.28)</i></p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-nagara-bhramiya-amara-gaura-elo-ghore" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="">
<h2>After parikramā <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<p class="verse"><strong>nagara bhramiyā āmāra gaura elo ghore</strong><br>
<strong>gaura elo ghore āmāra nitāi elo ghore</strong><br>
<strong>dhūlā jhāri śacīmātā gaura kole kore</strong><br>
<strong>ānandete bhakta-goṇe hari hari bole</strong></p>
<p class="translation">“After <i>parikramā</i> of the town, Gaurāṅga returns to His house, and Nitāi also comes to the house. Dusting off His body, Śachī Mātā takes Gaurāṅga upon her lap. Out of great spiritual joy all the devotees are chanting ‘Hari Hari Bol!’”</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-radha-krishna-bol-bol-bolo-re-sobai" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h2>Śrī Nagara Kīrtana 4 <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>Śrī Nāma (from Gītāvalī)</h3>
<h4>by Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur</h4>
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>‘rādhā-kṛṣṇa’ bol bol bolo re sobāi</strong><br>
(ei)<strong> śikṣā diyā, sab nadīyā</strong><br>
<strong>phirche neche’ gaura-nitāi</strong><br>
(miche)<strong> māyār bośe, jāccho bhese’,</strong><br>
<strong>khāccho hābuḍubu, bhāi</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Chant, chant “Rādhā-Krishna!” Everyone chant! When Lord Chaitanya and Lord Nityānanda came dancing through Nadia, They gave these teachings: Chant, chant “Rādhā-Krishna!” Everyone chant! You are caught up in a whirlpool of senseless action and are sinking lower and lower. Chant, chant “Rādhā-Krishna!” Everyone chant!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse">(jlv)<strong> kṛṣṇa-dās, e biśvās,</strong><br>
<strong>korle to' ār duḥkho nāi</strong><br>
(kṛṣṇa)<strong> bolbe jabe, pulak ha'be</strong><br>
<strong>jhorbe āṅkhi, boli tāi</strong></p>
<p class="translation">If you just understand that the spirit soul is the eternal servant of Krsna, you will never have any more sorrows. Chant Hare Krsna and your eyes will fill with tears and your body will feel transcendental shivering. Chant, chant “Rādhā-Krishna!” Everyone chant!</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse">(‘rādhā)<strong> kṛṣṇa’ bolo, saṅge chalo,</strong><br>
<strong>ei-mātra bhikhā chāi</strong><br>
(jāy)<strong> sakal' bipod bhaktivinod</strong><br>
<strong>bole, jakhon o-nām gāi</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Simply chant “Rādhā-Krishna!” and join with us. Those are the only alms we beg. Chant, chant “Rādhā-Krishna!” Everyone chant! “All dangers will be gone when that Name is chanted,” says Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur. Chant, chant “Rādhā-Krishna!” Everyone chant!</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-hari-bole-modera-gaura-elo" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="">
<h2>Śrī Nagara Kīrtana 8 <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>Śrī Nāma (from Gītāvalī)</h3>
<h4>by Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur</h4>
<p class="verse"><strong>‘hari’ bole’ modera gaura elo</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(Refrain:) Our Lord Gaura came chanting “Hari! Hari!”</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>elo re gaurāṅga-chānd preme</strong><br>
<strong>elothelo nitāi-advaita-saṅge godrume paśilo</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Lord Gaurāṅga-chānd came looking very disheveled, as if mad, due to ecstatic love of God, and along with Lord Nityānanda and Advaita Āchārya, He entered the land of Godruma.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>saṅkīrtana-rase mete nam bilāilo</strong><br>
<strong>nāmera hāṭe ese preme jagat bhāsāilo</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Deeply absorbed in the intoxicating mellows of sankirtan, He distributed the holy name. Having come to the Marketplace of the Holy Name, He caused the whole universe to swim in ecstatic love of God.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>godruma-vāsīr āj duḥkha dūre gelo</strong><br>
<strong>bhakta-vṛnda-saṅge āsi’ hāṭa jāgāilo</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Today all the miseries of the residents of Godruma-dvīpa have gone far away, for Lord Gaurāṅga, having come here along with all His devotees, caused the Marketplace of the Holy Name to appear here in Godruma.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>nādīya bhramite gorā elo nāmer hāṭe</strong><br>
<strong>gaura elo hāṭe, saṅge nitāi elo hāṭe</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Wandering all over the land of Nadia, Lord Gaura came to the Marketplace of the Holy Name. Lord Gaura came to the Marketplace along with Nitāi.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>nāche mātoyārā nitāi godrumera māṭhe</strong><br>
<strong>jagat mātāy nitāi premera mālasāṭe</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Maddened with ecstasy, Lord Nityānanda dances in the fields of Godruma. Due to ecstatic love of God, He moves His powerful arms like a challenging wrestler and thus overwhelms the universe in ecstasy.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="6">
<h5>6</h5>
<p class="verse">(torā dekhe’ jā’ re)<br>
<strong>advaitādi bhakta-vṛnda nāche ghāṭe ghāṭe</strong><br>
<strong>palāya duranta kali poḍiyā bibhrāṭe</strong></p>
<p class="translation">(Oh, all of you please go and see!) The Vaiṣṇavas headed by Advaita Āchārya are dancing along the banks of the Gaṅga from one bathing ghat to the next! At such a sight as this, the wicked personality Kali falls into great danger and thus runs for his life.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="7">
<h5>7</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>ki sukhe bhāsilo jīva gorāchānder nāṭe</strong><br>
<strong>dekhiyā śuniyā pāṣanḍīr buka phāṭe</strong></p>
<p class="translation">In what indescribable happiness did all living beings swim while Lord Gaurachandra was dancing? By seeing and hearing of such Pastimes, the hearts of the atheists break.</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>
<section class="category">
<h1>Compositions of Śrīla Śrīdhar Mahārāj</h1>
<section class="song" id="song-ha-ha-bhaktivinoda-thakkura-guroh" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h2>Śrīmad Bhakti Vinod-viraha Daśakam <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>Prayer in Separation of Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur</h3>
<h4>by Śrīla Bhakti Rakṣak Śrīdhar Dev-Goswāmī Mahārāj</h4>
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>hā hā bhaktivinoda-ṭhakkura! guroḥ!</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dvāviṁśatis te samā</strong><br>
<strong>dīrghād-duḥkha-bharād-aśeṣa-virahād-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;duḥsthīkṛtā bhūriyam</strong><br>
<strong>jīvānāṁ bahu-janma-puṇya-nivahā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kṛṣṭo mahī-maṇḍale</strong><br>
<strong>āvirbhāva-kṛpāṁ chakāra cha bhavān</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;śrī-gaura-śaktiḥ svayam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Alas, alas! Oh Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur, Oh Supreme Guru, for twenty-two years this world has been plunged into misfortune, long grief-stricken in your intolerable separation. You are the divine potency of Śrī Gaura, and you graciously made your advent in this earthly plane, being attracted by the living beings’ virtuous deeds performed throughout many lifetimes.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>dīno ’haṁ chira-duṣkṛtir na hi bhavat-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;pādābja-dhūli-kaṇā-</strong><br>
<strong>snānānanda-nidhiṁ prapanna-śubhadaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;labdhuṁ samartho ’bhavam</strong><br>
<strong>kintv audārya-guṇāt-tavāti-yaśasaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kāruṇya-śaktiḥ svayaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>śrī-śrī-gaura-mahāprabhoḥ prakaṭitā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;viśvaṁ samanvagrahīt</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Since I am lowly and very wretched, it was not my luck to reach the ocean that bestows the fortune of surrender — the ocean of the ecstasy of bathing in a particle of the dust of your holy lotus feet. Yet due to your magnanimous nature, you gave your grace to the whole universe, personally revealing your supereminence as the personification of the mercy potency of Śrī Gaurāṅga. (That is, by coming into this world, I received his grace.)</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>he deva! stavane tavākhila-guṇānāṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;te viriñchādayo</strong><br>
<strong>devā vyartha-mano-rathāḥ kim u vayaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;martyādhamāḥ kurmahe</strong><br>
<strong>etan no vibudhaiḥ kadāpy atiśayālaṅkāra</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ity uchyatāṁ</strong><br>
<strong>śāstreṣv eva “na pāraye ’ham” iti yad</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gātaṁ mukundena tat</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Oh Lord, even all the demigods headed by Lord Brahmā feel frustrated by their inability to (fittingly) sing the glories of all your divine qualities. What, then, can be said of the attempt of a fallen soul, a mere human like me? Surely, the learned will never waste their eloquent words eulogising this statement, since even the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Krishna Himself, has sung in the Scriptures His celebrated confession <i>na pāraye ’ham</i> — ‘I am unable (to reciprocate your devotion).’</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>dharmaś-charma-gato ’jñataiva satatā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;yogaś cha bhogātmako</strong><br>
<strong>jñāne śūnya-gatir japena tapasā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;khyātir jighāṁsaiva cha</strong><br>
<strong>dāne dāmbhikatā ’nurāga-bhajane</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;duṣṭāpachāro yadā</strong><br>
<strong>buddhiṁ buddhi-matāṁ vibheda hi tadā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dhātrā bhavān preṣitaḥ</strong></p>
<p class="translation">At a time when religion was evaluated in terms of bodily relationship, saintliness was determined by ignorance, and <i>yoga</i> practice was motivated by sensual pleasure; when scholarship was cultivated just for voidism, <i>japa</i> was conducted for the sake of fame, and penance was performed out of vindictiveness; when charity was given out of pride, and on the pretext of spontaneous devotion the most gross, sinful acts were perpetrated — and in all such anomalous situations even the intelligentsia were at loggerheads with one another... at that very time, you were sent by the Almighty Creator.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>viśve ’smin kiraṇair yathā hima-karaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sañjīvayann oṣadhīr</strong><br>
<strong>nakṣatrāṇi cha rañjayan nija-sudhāṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;vistārayan rājate</strong><br>
<strong>sach-chāstrāṇi cha toṣayan budha-gaṇaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sammodayaṁs te tathā</strong><br>
<strong>nūnaṁ bhūmi-tale śubhodaya iti</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;hlādo bahuḥ sātvatām</strong></p>
<p class="translation">As the universally cooling moon is beautified by diffusing its nectarean rays, nourishing the plants and inciting the twinkling of the stars, your Holy Advent in this world is similarly ascertained as satisfying the pure devotional Scriptures (through profound study) and blessing the learned with full-blown happiness (by the perfect axiomatic conclusions descending in Divine Succession). With your appearance, the ecstasy of the devotees knows no bounds.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="6">
<h5>6</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>lokānāṁ hita-kāmyayā bhagavato</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhakti-prachāras tvayā</strong><br>
<strong>granthānāṁ rachanaiḥ satām abhimatair</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nānā-vidhair darśitaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>āchāryaiḥ kṛta-pūrvam eva kila tad</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;rāmānujādyair budhaiḥ</strong><br>
<strong>premāmbho-nidhi-vigrahasya bhavato</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;māhātmya-sīmā na tat</strong></p>
<p class="translation">By writing many books and by multifarious methods recognised by the pure devotees, you have demonstrated the preaching of pure devotional service unto the Supreme Lord, for the benefit of the whole world. We have heard of similar achievements in previous times by stalwart scholars such as Śrī Rāmānuja and many other Āchāryas; but the glory of you — the very embodiment of the nectar of divine love — does not end (cannot be confined) here.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="7">
<h5>7</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>yad dhāmnaḥ khalu dhāma chaiva nigame</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;brahmeti saṁjñāyate</strong><br>
<strong>yasyāṁśasya kalaiva duḥkha-nikarair</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;yogeśvarair mṛgyate</strong><br>
<strong>vaikuṇṭhe para-mukta-bhṛṅga-charaṇo</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nārāyaṇo yaḥ svayaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>tasyāṁśī bhagavān svayaṁ rasa-vapuḥ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kṛṣṇo bhavān tat pradaḥ</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Merely the effulgence of His divine abode has been designated by the title ‘Brahman’ in the Vedas, and only the expansion of an expansion of His expansion is sought after with great tribulation by the foremost <i>yogīs</i>. The most exalted of the liberated souls shine resplendent as the bumblebees at His lotus feet. The Primeval Origin of even the Original Śrī Nārāyaṇa who is the Lord of the spiritual sky above Brahman: He is the Original Supreme Lord, the personification of all nectarean mellows — Śrī Krishna — and He is the one that you give.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="8">
<h5>8</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>sarvāchintyamaye parātpara-pure</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;goloka-vṛndāvane</strong><br>
<strong>chil-līlā-rasa-raṅginī parivṛtā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sā rādhikā śrī-hareḥ</strong><br>
<strong>vātsalyādi-rasaiś cha sevita-tanor-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;mādhurya-sevā-sukhaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>nityaṁ yatra mudā tanoti hi bhavān</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tad dhāma-sevā-pradaḥ</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Situated in the topmost region of the absolutely inconceivable spiritual sky is the holy abode of Śrī Vṛndāvan Dhām, in the spiritual planet known as Goloka. There, surrounded by Sakhīs, Śrīmatī Rādhikā revels in the mellows of divine Pastimes. With great ecstasy, She expands the joy of loving sweetness in the service of Śrī Krishnachandra, who is otherwise served in four relationships up to parenthood. You, Oh Ṭhākur Bhakti Vinod, can give us the service of that holy Dhām.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="9">
<h5>9</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-gaurānumataṁ svarūpa-viditaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;rūpāgrajenādṛtaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>rūpādyaiḥ pariveśitaṁ raghu-gaṇair</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;āsvāditaṁ sevitam</strong><br>
<strong>jīvādyair abhirakṣitaṁ śuka-śiva-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;brahmādi-sammānitaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>śrī-rādhā-pada-sevanāmṛtam aho</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tad dātum īśo bhavān</strong></p>
<p class="translation">The internal purport is known to Śrī Svarūp Dāmodar by the sanction of Śrī Gaurachandra, of that which is adored by Śrī Sanātan Goswāmī and distributed by the preceptors realised in transcendental mellows, headed by Śrī Rūpa Goswāmī; that which is tasted and enhanced by Śrī Raghunāth Dās Goswāmī and followers and carefully protected by the votaries headed by Śrī Jīva Prabhu; and that which (from a respectful distance) is venerated by great personalities such as Śrī Śuka, Lord Śiva, the chief of the demigods, and Lord Brahmā, the grandfather of all beings — Oh wonder of wonders! the nectarean rapture of servitude unto Śrī Rādhikā — that, too, you can give us.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="10">
<h5>10</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>kvāhaṁ manda-matis tv atīva-patitaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kva tvaṁ jagat-pāvanaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>bho svāmin kṛpayāparādha-nichayo</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nūnaṁ tvayā kṣamyatām</strong><br>
<strong>yāche ’haṁ karuṇā-nidhe! varam imaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;pādābja-mūle bhavat-</strong><br>
<strong>sarvasvāvadhi-rādhikā-dayita-dāsānāṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaṇe gaṇyatām</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Where am I, so lowly and fallen, and where are you, the great soul who delivers the universe! Oh Lord, by your grace, you are sure to forgive my offences. Oh ocean of mercy, in the dust of your lotus feet I pray for just this benediction: kindly make my life successful by recommending me for admission into the group of Śrī Vārṣabhānavī Dayita Dās, who is the dearmost one in your heart.<br>
Translator’s  Note: The preceding prayer to Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur (1838-1914), the pioneer of pure devotion in the recent age, was composed within the lifetime of the author’s Guru, Śrīla Bhakti Siddhānta Saraswatī Ṭhākur Goswāmī Prabhupād. Upon reading the original Sanskrit verse, Śrīla Prabhupād commented, “Now I am confident that we have one man who is qualified to uphold the standard of our Sampradāya (Divine Succession).”</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-guror-guro-me-paramo-gurus-tvam" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h2>Śrī Śrīmad Gaura-Kiśora- namaskāra Daśakam <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>Homage unto Śrīmad Gaura Kiśora Bābājī Mahārāj</h3>
<h4>by Śrīla Bhakti Rakṣak Śrīdhar Dev-Goswāmī Mahārāj</h4>
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>guror guro me paramo gurus tvaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>vareṇya! gaurāṅga-gaṇāgragaṇye</strong><br>
<strong>prasīda bhṛtye dayitāśrite te</strong><br>
<strong>namo namo gaura-kiśora tubhyam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Oh Divine Master of my Divine Master, my most venerable preceptor, you are supremely worshippable in the group of the foremost associates of Śrī Gaurāṅga. May you be gracious upon this servitor surrendered unto your loving servitor (Dayita Dās). Oh Gaura Kiśora, again and again do I make my obeisance unto you.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>sarasvatī-nāma-jagat-prasiddhaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>prabhuṁ jagatyāṁ patitaika-bandhum</strong><br>
<strong>tvam eva deva! prakaṭī-chakāra</strong><br>
<strong>namo namo gaura-kiśora tubhyam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Oh worshipful one, you alone revealed my Lord and Master, who is renowned throughout the universe as Śrī Bhakti Siddhānta Saraswatī, and who is the only friend of the fallen souls of the world. Oh Gaura Kiśora, again and again do I make my obeisance unto you.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>kvachid-vrajāraṇya-vivikta-vāsī</strong><br>
<strong>hṛdi vraja-dvandva-raho-vilāsī</strong><br>
<strong>bahir virāgī tv avadhūta-veṣī</strong><br>
<strong>namo namo gaura-kiśora tubhyam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Living in solitude at Vraja Dhām, your heart was absorbed in the most secret Pastimes of the Divine Youthful Couple of Vraja, whilst externally you maintained the strictures of a renunciate; and sometimes you appeared to transcend all recognised strictures. Oh Gaura Kiśora, again and again do I make my obeisance unto you.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>kvachit punar gaura-vanāntachārī</strong><br>
<strong>surāpagā-tīra-rajo-vihārī</strong><br>
<strong>pavitra-kaupīna-karaṅka-dhārī</strong><br>
<strong>namo namo gaura-kiśora tubhyam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Sometimes you roam about the edge of Gauravana (the boundary of Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām), wandering along the beach near the banks of the Gaṅgā. Oh Gaura Kiśora, who dons the holy loin-cloth and carries the mendicant’s water-pot, again and again do I make my obeisance unto you.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>sadā harer nāma mudā raṭantaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>gṛhe gṛhe mādhukarīm aṭantam</strong><br>
<strong>namanti devā api yaṁ mahāntaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>namo namo gaura-kiśora tubhyam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Ever singing the Holy Name of Śrī Hari with great ecstasy and accepting alms from house to house like a bee collecting honey from flower to flower, you are the great soul unto whom even the demigods bow to. Oh Gaura Kiśora, again and again do I make my obeisance unto you.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="6">
<h5>6</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>kvachid-rudantañ cha hasan naṭantaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>nijeṣṭa-deva-praṇayābhibhūtam</strong><br>
<strong>namanti gāyantam alaṁ janā tvāṁ</strong><br>
<strong>namo namo gaura-kiśora tubhyam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Becoming overwhelmed with love for your worshipful Lord, sometimes you dance, sometimes you cry, sometimes you laugh; and again, you sing aloud. The people profusely offer their respects unto you, Oh Gaura Kiśora, and again and again do I make my obeisance unto you.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="7">
<h5>7</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>mahāyaśo-bhaktivinoda-bandho!</strong><br>
<strong>mahāprabhu-prema-sudhaika-sindho!</strong><br>
<strong>aho jagannātha-dayāspadendo!</strong><br>
<strong>namo namo gaura-kiśora tubhyam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Oh friend of the glorious Ṭhākur Bhakti Vinod, Oh matchless ocean of the nectar of loving devotion for Mahāprabhu Śrī Chaitanyadev, Oh moon that received the grace of Vaiṣṇava Sārvabhauma Śrī Jagannāth, Oh Gaura Kiśora! again and again do I make my obeisance unto you.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="8">
<h5>8</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>samāpya rādhā-vratam uttamaṁ tvam</strong><br>
<strong>avāpya dāmodara-jāgarāham</strong><br>
<strong>gato ’si rādhādara-sakhya-riddhiṁ</strong><br>
<strong>namo namo gaura-kiśora tubhyam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Completing the great holy vow of Ūrja-vrata, you selected the day of the awakening of Śrī Dāmodar to achieve the cherished treasure of your internal identity as a Sakhī devoted to the service of Śrī Rādhikā. Oh Gaura-Kiśora, again and again do I make my obeisance unto you.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="9">
<h5>9</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>vihāya saṅgaṁ kuliyā-layānāṁ</strong><br>
<strong>pragṛhya sevāṁ dayitānugasya</strong><br>
<strong>vibhāsi māyāpura-mandira-stho</strong><br>
<strong>namo namo gaura-kiśora tubhyam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Forsaking the company of the residents of Kuliyā town to accept the service of your servitor Śrī Dayita Dās, your divine presence is now found in a holy temple at Śrī Dhām Māyāpur. Oh Gaura Kiśora, again and again do I make obeisance unto you.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="10">
<h5>10</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>sadā nimagno ’py aparādha-paṅke</strong><br>
<strong>hy ahaitukīm eṣa kṛpāñ cha yāche</strong><br>
<strong>dayāṁ samuddhṛtya vidhehi dīnaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>namo namo gaura-kiśora tubhyam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Although I remain deep in the mud of offences, I (a fallen soul) am begging you for your causeless mercy. Please be gracious and deliver this soul bereft. Oh Gaura Kiśora, again and yet again do I make my obeisance unto you.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-nite-yasmin-nishiante-nayana-jala-bharaih" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h2>Śrī Śrī Dayita Dās Daśakam <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>Prayer unto Śrīla Saraswatī Ṭhākur after his manifest Pastimes</h3>
<h4>by Śrīla Bhakti Rakṣak Śrīdhar Dev-Goswāmī Mahārāj</h4>
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>nīte yasmin niśānte nayana-jala-bharaiḥ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;snāta-gātrārvudānāṁ</strong><br>
<strong>uchchair utkrośatāṁ śrī-vṛṣakapi-sutayā-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dhīrayā svīya-goṣṭhīm</strong><br>
<strong>pṛthvī gāḍhāndhakārair hṛta-nayana-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;maṇīvāvṛtā yena hīnā</strong><br>
<strong>yatrāsau tatra śīghraṁ kṛpaṇa-nayana he</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nīyatāṁ kiṅkaro ’yam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">At the end of the night of Śrī Śrī Vṛṣabhānunandinī, She suddenly took him to Her entourage, withdrawing him from the company of throngs of griefstricken souls. A great cry of lamentation arose, their bodies were bathed by their tears. When he was thus stolen away, this world was plunged into the deep darkness of one whose eyes have been stolen away (<i>hṛta</i>—stolen away;<i>nayana-maṇi</i>—jewel of the eye—the internal name of Saraswatī Ṭhākur is ‘Nayana-maṇi’).<br>
(Bereft of the vision of my Divine Master,) Oh my sorrowful eyes (<i>dīna-nayana</i>), (or, Oh saviour of the fallen [Dīna-nayana],) wherever that great soul may be, please quickly take this servitor there! (Although he is my Master named ‘Nayana,’ or ‘one who brings us near,’ in his mercy he shows the miserliness of not taking me unto his company.)</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>yasya śrī-pāda-padmāt pravahati jagati</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;prema-pīyūṣa-dhārā</strong><br>
<strong>yasya śrī-pāda-padma-chyuta-madhu satataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhṛtya-bhṛṅgān vibharti</strong><br>
<strong>yasya śrī-pāda-padmaṁ vraja-rasika-jano</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;modate sampraśasya</strong><br>
<strong>yatrāsau tatra śīghraṁ kṛpaṇa-nayana he</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nīyatāṁ kiṅkaro ’yam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">From his lotus feet, the nectarean river of divine love flows throughout the universe; his servitors, like bees, maintain their lives drinking the honey that falls from his lotus feet; and the pure devotees in the shelter of the confidential mellows of Vraja revel in the bliss of singing the glories of his lotus feet: Oh Dīna-nayana, wherever that great soul may be, please quickly take this servitor there.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>vātsalyaṁ yach cha pitro jagati bahumataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kaitavaṁ kevalaṁ tat</strong><br>
<strong>dāmpatyaṁ dasyutaiva svajana-gaṇa-kṛtā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bandhutā vañchaneti</strong><br>
<strong>vaikuṇṭha-sneha-mūrteḥ pada-nakha-kiraṇair</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;yasya sandarśito ’smi</strong><br>
<strong>yatrāsau tatra śīghraṁ kṛpaṇa-nayana he</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nīyatāṁ kiṅkaro ’yam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Parental affection, so highly esteemed in the world, is a colossal hoax (as an obstacle to Hari-bhakti); socially recognised pure matrimonial love is nothing but dacoity (in that it plunders away both the husband’s and the wife’s eagerness for the chance to acquire the treasure of love unadulterated by the superficiality of familial prejudice); and common friendship is merely deception: I have gleaned these thoughts from the rays of light that emanate from the toenails of the holy feet of that great personality, the embodiment of supramundane affection. Oh Dīna-nayana, wherever that great soul may be, please quickly take this servitor there.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>yā vāṇī kaṇṭha-lagnā vilasati satataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kṛṣṇa-chaitanyachandre</strong><br>
<strong>karṇa-kroḍāj-janānāṁ kim u nayana-gatāṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;saiva mūrtiṁ prakāśya</strong><br>
<strong>nīlādrī-śasya netrārpaṇa-bhavana-gatā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;netra-tārābhidheyā</strong><br>
<strong>yatrāsau tatra śīghraṁ kṛpaṇa-nayana he</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nīyatāṁ kiṅkaro ’yam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">The divine message sung by Śrī Krishna Chaitanya-chandra had its continuous play in the ears of the people. From the ear, did he, just to fulfil the purport of the name ‘Nayana-maṇi,’ reveal his form to the eye, making his advent in the mansion (temple) favoured by the glance of Śrī Nīlāchalachandra (at the time of the Ratha Yātrā festival)? Oh Dīna-nayana, wherever that great soul (Mahāpuruṣa) may be, please swiftly take this servitor there.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>gaurendor asta-śaile kim u kanaka-ghano</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;hema-hṛj-jambu-nadyā</strong><br>
<strong>āvirbhūtaḥ pravarṣair nikhila-jana-padaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;plāvayan dāva-dagdham</strong><br>
<strong>gaurāvirbhāva-bhūmau rajasi cha sahasā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;saṁjugopa svayaṁ svaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>yatrāsau tatra śīghraṁ kṛpaṇa-nayana he</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nīyatāṁ kiṅkaro ’yam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Drawing up the pure golden waters of the Jambu River mentioned in Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, has this golden cloud arisen on the mountain where the golden moon (Śrī Gaurachandra) descended (set), just to shower down torrents of rain upon the whole countryside that was scorched by the forest fire (of threefold sufferings), then suddenly conceal himself in the dust of the land of the Advent of Śrī Gaurāṅga? Oh Dīna-nayana, wherever that Great Master may be now, please quickly take this servitor to that place.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="6">
<h5>6</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>gauro gaurasya śiṣyo gurur api jagatāṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gāyatāṁ gaura-gāthā</strong><br>
<strong>gauḍe gauḍīya-goṣṭhy-āśrita-gaṇa-garimā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;drāviḍe gaura-garvī</strong><br>
<strong>gāndharvā gaura-vāṭyo giri-dhara-parama-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;preyasāṁ yo gariṣṭho</strong><br>
<strong>yatrāsau tatra śīghraṁ kṛpaṇa-nayana he</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nīyatāṁ kiṅkaro ’yam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">He is of the same hue as Śrī Gaura, and although he, who sings the tidings of Śrī Gaura, is the (natural) Guru of the whole universe, he accepted discipleship to a great devotee called Śrī Gaura Kiśora (‘adolescent Gaura’). In the whole Gaura Maṇḍal he is the repository of the glory of those who grant entrance into the fold of the pure Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas. He proudly ascended the lofty throne of preaching the glories of the gift of the Golden Lord Śrī Gaura (the service of Śrī Rādhā-Govinda in Vraja) to the Vaiṣṇavas of Draviḍa (the devotees in South India, who are generally worshippers of Lakṣmī-Nārāyaṇa). The glory of his dignity shines even in the group of Śrī Gāndharvā, and he holds a pre-eminent position in the intimate circle of Śrī Giridhārī, that is, he is the most beloved of Lord Mukunda. Oh Dīna-nayana, where that great soul is now, please swiftly take this servitor there.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="7">
<h5>7</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>yo rādhā-kṛṣṇa-nāmāmṛta-jala-nidhinā-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;plāvayad-viśvam etad</strong><br>
<strong>āmlechchhāśeṣa-lokaṁ dvija-nṛpa-vaṇijaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;śūdra-śūdrāpakṛṣṭam</strong><br>
<strong>muktaiḥ siddhair agamyaḥ patita-jana-sakho</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaura-kāruṇya-śaktir</strong><br>
<strong>yatrāsau tatra śīghraṁ kṛpaṇa-nayana he</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nīyatāṁ kiṅkaro ’yam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">He inundated the whole universe of countless living beings—whether <i>brāhmaṇ</i>, <i>kṣatriya</i>, <i>vaiśya</i>, <i>śūdra</i>, less than <i>śūdra</i>, and even <i>mlechchha</i>—with the oceanic nectar of the Holy Name of Śrī Rādhā-Krishna. Although unapproachable by the liberationists and <i>yogic</i> perfectionists, he is known as the friend of the fallen, the mercy potency of Śrī Gaurāṅga. Oh Dīna-nayana, wherever that great soul may be, please swiftly take this servitor there.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="8">
<h5>8</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>apy āśā vartate tat puraṭa-vara-vapur</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;lokituṁ loka-śandaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>dīrghaṁ nīlābja-netraṁ tila-kusuma-nasaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ninditārdhendu-bhālam</strong><br>
<strong>saumyaṁ śubhrāṁśu-dantaṁ śata-dala-vadanaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dīrgha-bāhuṁ vareṇyaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>yatrāsau tatra śīghraṁ kṛpaṇa-nayana he</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nīyatāṁ kiṅkaro ’yam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Do I have a hope to ever see that beautiful golden personality who makes everything auspicious for the world? Do I have a hope to ever behold that tall figure once again, his blue lotus eyes, his nose that excels the charm of the Tila flower, his forehead that shames the half-moon, his graceful lotus face, his gleaming pearlwhite teeth, his long arms extending to his knees? Oh Dīna-nayana, wherever that great soul has gone, please swiftly take this servitor there.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="9">
<h5>9</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>gaurābde śūnya-bāṇānvita-nigamamite</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kṛṣṇa-pakṣe chaturthyāṁ</strong><br>
<strong>pauṣe māse maghāyām amara-gaṇa-guror-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;vāsare vai niśānte</strong><br>
<strong>dāso yo rādhikāyā atiśaya-dayito</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nitya-līlā-praviṣṭo</strong><br>
<strong>yatrāsau tatra śīghraṁ kṛpaṇa-nayana he</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nīyatāṁ kiṅkaro ’yam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">In the year of our Lord Gaurāṅga 450, in the month of Pauṣa, on the fourth day of the dark fortnight of the moon in the star of Maghā, at the closing of the night of Bṛhaspativara (5.30 a.m., 1st January, 1937), that most beloved attendant of Śrīmatī Vṛṣabhānunandinī entered into the eternal Pastimes. Oh Dīna-nayana, wherever that great soul may be, quickly take this servitor there to him.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="10">
<h5>10</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>hā-hā-kārair-janānāṁ guru-charaṇa-juṣāṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;pūritābhūr-nabhaś cha</strong><br>
<strong>yāto ’sau kutra viśvaṁ prabhupada-virahādd-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;hanta śūnyāyitaṁ me</strong><br>
<strong>pādābje nitya-bhṛtyaḥ kṣaṇam api virahaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;notsahe soḍhum atra</strong><br>
<strong>yatrāsau tatra śīghraṁ kṛpaṇa-nayana he</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nīyatāṁ kiṅkaro ’yam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">The entire earth and skies were filled with the cries of anguish of the people, and of the disciples devoted to the service of Śrī Gurudeva’s lotus feet. Where has that Great Master gone? Alas! today the whole universe seems empty, in the separation of Prabhupād. The servitor of Gurudeva cannot endure even a moment of his separation. Oh Dīna-nayana, wherever, wherever that great soul may be, please quickly take this servitor there to him.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-shrimach-chaitanyapadau-chara" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h2>Śrīmad Rūpa-pada-rajaḥ Prārthanā Daśakam <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>Aspiring for the Dust of Śrīmad Rūpa Goswāmī’s Lotus Feet</h3>
<h4>by Śrīla Bhakti Rakṣak Śrīdhar Dev-Goswāmī Mahārāj</h4>
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrīmach-chaitanyapādau chara-kamalayugau</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;netra-bhṛṅgau madhu dyau</strong><br>
<strong>gauḍe tau pāyayantau vraja-vipina-gatau</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;vyājayuktau samutkau</strong><br>
<strong>bhātau sabhrātṛkasya svajana-gaṇa-pater</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;yasya saubhāgya-bhūmnaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>sa śrī-rūpaḥ kadā māṁ nija-pada-rajasā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhūṣitaṁ saṁvidhatte</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Śrīla Rūpa Goswāmī is the mine of the most precious treasure, the leader of the associates of Lord Chaitanya (the Divine Succession being known as the Rūpānuga Sampradāya, ‘the followers of Śrī Rūpa’). In the company of his brothers, he was resplendent in (the province of) Gauḍa, causing the fervent twin bumblebees in the form of the lotus eyes of Śrī Chaitanyadev to drink nectar, as the Lord’s lotus feet moved on the pretext of visiting Vṛndāvan. When will that Śrīmad Rūpa Prabhu grace me with the dust of his holy feet?</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>pīta-śrī-gaura-pādāmbuja-madhu-madiron-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;matta-hṛd-bhṛṅga-rājo</strong><br>
<strong>rājyaiśvaryaṁ jahau yo jana-nivaha-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;hitādatta-chitto nijāgryam</strong><br>
<strong>vijñāpya svānujena vraja-gamana-rataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;chānvagāt gaurachandraṁ</strong><br>
<strong>sa śrī-rūpaḥ kadā māṁ nija-pada-rajasā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhūṣitaṁ saṁvidhatte</strong></p>
<p class="translation">At Śrī Rāmakeli Dhām, the king of bees—the heart of Śrī Rūpa—became intoxicated drinking the honey-wine of the lotus feet of Śrī Gaurachandra, and sacrificed its life (in Hari-kīrtan) for the benefit of all the people of the world, summarily abandoning a life of kingly opulence. After informing his elder brother Śrī Sanātan, Śrī Rūpa and his younger brother Śrī Vallabha followed the footsteps of Śrī Chaitanyadev, whose heart was absorbed in going (from Nīlāchala) to Śrī Vṛndāvan. When will that Śrī Rūpa Prabhu grace me with the dust of his holy lotus feet?</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>vṛndāraṇyāt prayāge hari-rasa-naṭanair</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nāma-saṅkīrtanaiś cha</strong><br>
<strong>lebhe yo mādhavāgre jana-gahana-gataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;prema-mattaṁ janāṁś cha</strong><br>
<strong>bhāvaiḥ svair mādayantaṁ hṛta-nidhir iva</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;taṁ kṛṣṇa-chaitanyachandraṁ</strong><br>
<strong>sa śrī-rūpaḥ kadā māṁ nija-pada-rajasā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhūṣitaṁ saṁvidhatte</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Śrī  Chaitanyadev  returned  from  Vṛndāvan to  Prayāga  Dhām  and  performed  Nām-saṅkīrtan, surrounded  by  crowds  of  hundreds  of  thousands. Intoxicated with divine love, dancing, He melted the hearts of hundreds of faithful souls with His astonishing transcendental ecstasies. Śrī Rūpa, as though regaining his most precious treasure, thus found Śrī Chaitanyadev in the direct presence of the Deity Śrī Bindu Mādhava. When will that Śrī Rūpa Prabhu grace me with the dust of his holy lotus feet?</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>ekāntaṁ labdha-pādāmbuja-nija-hṛdaya-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;preṣṭha-pātro mahārtir</strong><br>
<strong>dainyair-duḥkhāśru-pūrṇair daśana-dhṛta-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tṛṇaiḥ pūjayāmāsa gauram</strong><br>
<strong>svāntaḥ kṛṣṇañ cha gaṅgā-dinamaṇi-tanayā-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;saṅgame sānujo yaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>sa śrī-rūpaḥ kadā māṁ nija-pada-rajasā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhūṣitaṁ saṁvidhatte</strong></p>
<p class="translation">At the sacred spot on the confluence of the Gaṅgā and the Yamunā, Śrī Rūpa achieved intimate union with the lotus feet of the dearmost Lord of his life—the Lord who is Krishna within and Gaura without. In grave humility, with straw in mouth and with tears of sorrow, he along with his younger brother worshipped Śrī Gaura-Krishna in intense love. When will that Śrī Rūpa Prabhu grace me with the dust of his holy lotus feet?</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>svasya prema-svarūpaṁ priya-dayita-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;vilāsānurūpaika-rūpaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>dūre bhū-luṇṭhitaṁ yaṁ sahaja-sumadhura-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;śrīyutaṁ sānujañ cha</strong><br>
<strong>dṛṣṭvā devo ’titūrṇaṁ stuti-bahu-mukham</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;āśliṣya gāḍhaṁ rarañje</strong><br>
<strong>sa śrī-rūpaḥ kadā māṁ nija-pada-rajasā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhūṣitaṁ saṁvidhatte</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Śrī Chaitanyadev saw Śrī Rūpa and his younger brother rolling on the ground some distance away. Seeing His endeared one, His favourite, His love divine personified in natural beauty and charm, His exclusive alter ego in divine Pastimes, the Lord swiftly approached Śrī Rūpa, profusely singing his glories, and embraced him in ecstasy. When will that Śrī Rūpa Prabhu grace me with the dust of his holy lotus feet?</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="6">
<h5>6</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>kaivalya-prema-bhūmāv-akhila-rasa-sudhā-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sindhu-sañchāra-dakṣaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>jñātvāpy evañ cha rādhā-pada-bhajana-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sudhāṁ līlayāpāyayad yam</strong><br>
<strong>śaktiṁ sañchārya gauro nija-bhajana-sudhā-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dāna-dakṣaṁ chakāra</strong><br>
<strong>sa śrī-rūpaḥ kadā māṁ nija-pada-rajasā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhūṣitaṁ saṁvidhatte</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Śrī Gaurahari knew that (as an eternal associate of the Lord) Śrī Rūpa was already proficient in wandering throughout the ambrosial ocean of all mellows, in the land of unalloyed love (in Vraja-rasa). Nonetheless, to expand His own Pastimes, the Lord enabled him to drink the sweet ecstasy of servitude unto Śrī Rādhā, and empowered him with the skill to distribute the nectar of His personal devotional service. When will that Śrīmad Rūpa Prabhu grace me with the dust of his lotus feet?</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="7">
<h5>7</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>gaurādeśāch cha vṛndā-vipinam iha</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;parikramya nīlāchalaṁ yo</strong><br>
<strong>gatvā kāvyāmṛtaiḥ svair vraja-yuva-yugala-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;krīḍanārthaiḥ prakāmam</strong><br>
<strong>rāmānanda-svarūpādibhir api kavibhis</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tarpayāmāsa gauraṁ</strong><br>
<strong>sa śrī-rūpaḥ kadā māṁ nija-pada-rajasā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhūṣitaṁ saṁvidhatte</strong></p>
<p class="translation">When on the order of Śrī Gaurāṅga, Śrī Rūpa visited Śrī Puruṣottama Kṣetra after completing the circumambulation of Śrī Vraja Maṇḍal, He highly gratified Śrī Chaitanyadev and the sagacious devotee assembly headed by Śrī Svarūp Dāmodar and Śrī Rāmānanda Rāya by his ambrosial poetry on the Pastimes of the Divine Couple of Vraja. When will that Śrīmad Rūpa Prabhu grace me with the dust of his lotus feet?</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="8">
<h5>8</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>līlā-saṁgopane śrī-bhagavata iha vai</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;jaṅgame sthāvare ’pi</strong><br>
<strong>saṁmugdhe sāgrajātaḥ prabhu-viraha-hṛta-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;prāya-jīvendriyāṇām</strong><br>
<strong>yaś chāsīd āśrayaika-sthalam iva raghu-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gopāla-jīvādi-varge</strong><br>
<strong>sa śrī-rūpaḥ kadā māṁ nija-pada-rajasā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhūṣitaṁ saṁvidhatte</strong></p>
<p class="translation">When the manifest līlā of the Supreme Lord Śrī Chaitanyadev was withdrawn, all beings including the immobile entities were mortified, deeply afflicted in sorrow. Śrī Rūpa and his elder brother were the only shelter for even the intimate devotees of the Lord, headed by Raghunāth, Gopal Bhaṭṭa, and Śrī Jīva, who had almost lost their lives in the Lord’s separation. When will that Śrīmad Rūpa Prabhu grace me with the dust of his lotus feet?</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="9">
<h5>9</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-mūrteḥ sādhu-vṛtteḥ prakaṭanam api</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tal-lupta-tīrthādikānāṁ</strong><br>
<strong>śrī-rādhā-kṛṣṇa-pādāmbuja-bhajanamayaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;rāga-mārgaṁ viśuddham</strong><br>
<strong>granthair yena pradattaṁ nikhilam iha</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nijābhīṣṭa-devepsitañ cha</strong><br>
<strong>sa śrī rūpaḥ kadā māṁ nija-pada-rajasā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhūṣitaṁ saṁvidhatte</strong></p>
<p class="translation">By writing many, many books, he gave the world all the most cherished desires of his worshipful Lord Śrī Chaitanyadev, principally—revealing the service of the Deity, establishing the pure code of conduct in devotion, revealing the lost holy places, and revealing the path of spontaneous devotion (Rāga-mārga) in transcendental loving service to Śrī Rādhā-Govinda. When will that Śrīmad Rūpa Prabhu grace me with the dust of his holy lotus feet?</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="10">
<h5>10</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>līlā-saṁgopa-kāle nirupadhi-karuṇā-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kāriṇā svāmināhaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>yat pādābje ’rpito yat pada-bhajanamayaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gāyayitvā tu gītam</strong><br>
<strong>yogyāyogyatva-bhāvaṁ mama khalu sakalaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;duṣṭa-buddher agṛhṇan</strong><br>
<strong>sa śrī-rūpaḥ kadā māṁ nija-pada-rajasā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhūṣitaṁ saṁvidhatte</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Just prior to the withdrawal of his manifest līlā, my causelessly merciful Divine Master, Śrīla Saraswatī Ṭhākur, handed me over to the holy feet of that Divine Personality by having me sing the glorious prayer unto his lotus feet (Śrī Rūpa-mañjarī-pada). Despite my lowliness, when will—disregarding all my various qualifications and disqualifications—Śrīmad Rūpa Prabhu grace me with the dust of his holy lotus feet?</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-yo-nanto-nanta-vaktrair-niravadhi" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h2>Śrīman Nityānanda Dvādaśakam <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>12 Prayers revealing the ontological position and Pastimes of Lord Nityananda.</h3>
<h4>by Śrīla Bhakti Rakṣak Śrīdhar Dev-Goswāmī Mahārāj</h4>
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>yo ’nanto ’nanta-vaktrair niravadhi</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;hari-saṁkīrtanaṁ saṁvidhatte</strong><br>
<strong>yo vā dhatte dharitrīṁ śirasi</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;niravadhi kṣudra-dhūlī kaṇeva</strong><br>
<strong>yaḥ śeśaś-chatra-śayyāsana-vasana-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;vidhaiḥ sevate te yad-arthāḥ</strong><br>
<strong>śrī-nityānanda-chandraṁ bhaja bhaja satataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaura-kṛṣṇa-pradaṁ tam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Lord Nityānanda’s representation as Ananta Śeṣa posseses unlimited mouths which propagate and glorify Lord Hari’s Holy Names and attributes. Ananta Śeṣa constantly holds the weight of the Earth planet upon His innumerable hoods as if it were an insignificant particle of dust. Ananta Śeṣa serves the Lord in several ways, manifesting  Himself as the Lord’s personal umbrella, resting place, and clothing. Let us eternally worship that great personality Śrī Nityānanda-chandra, who freely grants the association of Śrī Gaurāṅgadev, who is none other than Lord Krishna.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>aṁśair yaḥ kṣīraśāyī sakala bhuvanapaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sarva jīvāntarastho</strong><br>
<strong>yo vā garbhodaśāyī-daśa-śata-vadano</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;veda-sūktair vigītaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>bramāṇḍāśeṣa garbhā prakṛti-pati-patir</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;jīva-saṅghāśrayāṅgaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>śrī nityānanda-chandraṁ bhaja bhaja satataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaura-kṛṣṇa-pradaṁ tam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Lord Nityānanda’s partial manifestation as Kśīrodakaśāyī Viṣṇu functions as thep reservation principle of the entire universal creation, and is localized within every soul (Paramātma). Another eminent expansion of Lord Nityānanda is Garbhodaśāyī Viṣṇu, that allimposing figure whose thousands of heads are eulogized in the Vedic orations. Lord Nityānanda’s presence is further extended in His expansion as Kāraṇodaśāyī Viṣṇu, the supplying principle encompassing unlimited universes. Lord Nityānanda is the master of the Lord of material nature, Kāraṇodaśāyī Viṣṇu, who is the shelter of all living entities. Let us eternally worship that great personality Śrī Nityānanda-chandra, who freely grants the association of Śrī Gaurāṅgadev, who is none other than Lord Krishna.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>yasyāṁśo vyuha-madhye vilasati parama-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;vyomni saṁkarṣaṇākhya</strong><br>
<strong>ātanvan śuddha-sattvaṁ nikhila-hari-sukhaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;chetanaṁ līlayā cha</strong><br>
<strong>jīvāhaṅkāra-bhāvāspada iti kathitaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kutrachij-jīvavad yaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>śrī-nityānanda-chandraṁ bhaja bhaja satataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaura-kṛṣṇa-pradaṁ tam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Lord Nityānanda’s presence is further extended within the spiritual abode of Vaikuṇṭha in His partial manifestation known as Saṅkarṣaṇa. In Vaikuṇṭha, Lord Saṅkarṣaṇa performs spiritual Pastimes which are fully transcendental, situated in pure goodness, and all-pleasing to Lord Hari. As mentioned in the revealed scriptures, Lord Nityānanda is the individual temperament of ego existing within the living entity, and He sometimes appears just like an ordinary living entity. Let us eternally worship that great personality Śrī Nityānanda-chandra, who freely grants the association of Śrī Gaurāṅgadev, who is none other than Lord Krishna.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>yaśchādi vyuha-madhye prabhavati sagaṇo</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;mūla-saṅkarṣaṇākhyo</strong><br>
<strong>dvārāvatyāṁ tad-ūrdhe madhupuri vasati</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;prābhavākhyo vilāsaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>sarvāṁśī rāma-nāmā vrajapuri ramate</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sānujo yaḥ svarūpe</strong><br>
<strong>śrī-nityānanda-chandraṁ bhaja bhaja satataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaura-kṛṣṇa-pradaṁ tam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Lord Nityānanda who is the fountainhead of the primary Saṅkarṣaṇa, exists in full splendour separate from the original quadruple expansion, and is known as Mūla Saṅkarṣaṇa. That all-attractive personality, Śrī Saṅkarṣaṇa., performs His wonderful Pastimes in the spiritual realm in the regions known as Mathurā and Dvārakā. The trascendental city of Dvārakā is situated just below the city of Mathurā. It is in these spiritual places that Lord Saṅkarṣaṇa and His associates Aniruddha and Pradyumna exhibit Their particular Pastimes scripturally known as prabhāva-vilāsaḥ. Nityānanda Prabhu in the form of Lord Balarāma is the ultimate source of all incarnations, and sportively plays in the charming village of Vrajapura with His younger brother Lord Krishna. Let us eternally worship that great personality Śrī Nityānanda-chandra, who freely grants the association of Śrī Gaurāṅgadev, who is none other than Lord Krishna.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-kṛṣṇa-premanāmā parama-sukhamayaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;ko’pyachintyaḥ padārtho</strong><br>
<strong>yad gandhāt sajjanaughā nigama-baṅhumataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;mokṣam apy ākṣipanti</strong><br>
<strong>kaivalyaiśvarya-sevā-pradagaṇa iti</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;yasyāṅgataḥ premadātuḥ</strong><br>
<strong>śrī nityānanda-chandraṁ bhaja bhaja satataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaura-kṛṣṇa-pradaṁ tam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Pure unalloyed love for Lord Krishna, which is inconceivable to all, embodies the most supreme and intense happiness. When the devotees of the Lord realize the joy of such love, they caste away the innumerable doctrines offered by the Vedas, including the soul’s emancipation. Lord Nityānandaopenlybestowspurelove, automatically including worship of the non-differentiated Brahman and service offered in Vaikuṇṭha, which is dominated by opulence and grandeur. Let us eternally worship that great personality Śrī Nityānanda-chandra, who freely grants the association of Śrī Gaurāṅgadev, who is none other than Lord Krishna.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="6">
<h5>6</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>yo bālye līlayaikaḥ parama-madhurayā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;chaikachakrānagaryāṁ</strong><br>
<strong>mātā-pitror janānā matha nija-suhṛdāṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;hlādayaṁś chitta-chakram</strong><br>
<strong>tīrthān vabhrāma sarvānupahṛta janako</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;nyāsinā prārthitaś cha</strong><br>
<strong>śrī-nityānanda-chandraṁ bhaja bhaja satataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaura-kṛṣṇa-pradaṁ tam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">When Nityānanda Prabhu was a small boy, He performed sweet and charming Pastimes in the village of Ekachakra. His Pastimes invoked the deepest heartfelt happiness in His mother, father, relatives, and close friends. Once, a renunciate arrived at the house of Nityānanda and prayed to the boy’s father for his son. Lord Nityānanda was placed in the hands of the renunciate and left to visit the holy places of pilgrimage, wandering throughout the length and breadth of India. Let us eternally worship that great personality Śrī Nityānanda-chandra, who freely grants the association of Śrī Gaurāṅgadev, who is none other than Lord Krishna.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="7">
<h5>7</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>bhrāmaṁ bhrāmañ cha tīrthān yati-mukuṭa-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;maṇi mādhavendra prasaṅgāt</strong><br>
<strong>labdhollāsaḥ pratikṣya prakaṭita-charitaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gauradhāmājagāma</strong><br>
<strong>śrī-gauraḥ śrīnivāsādibhir api</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;yamāvāpālaye nandanasya</strong><br>
<strong>śrī nityānanda-chandraṁ bhaja bhaja satataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaura-kṛṣṇa-pradaṁ tam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Nityānanda travelled from place to place with the crest jewel of the renounced order, Śrī Mādhavendra Purī. In the pure association of Mādhavendra Purī, Lord Nityānanda experienced the most joyful symptoms of ecstasy. After this, the Lord journeyed to Nabadwīp, where He eagerly waited for Śrī Chaitanya Mahāprabhu to reveal His benevolent nature to the world. Shortly following Nityānanda’s arrival. Lord Chaitanyadeva Himself, in the company of Śrīnivās Paṇḍit and Haridās Ṭhākur, went to the house of Śrī Nandanāchārya and found Nitāi there. Let us eternally worship that great personality Śrī Nityānanda-chandra, who freely grants the association of Śrī Gaurāṅgadev, who is none other than Lord Krishna.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="8">
<h5>8</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>prāptājño gaura-chandrādakhilajana</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaṇoddhāra-nāma-pradāne</strong><br>
<strong>yaḥ prāpya dvau surāpau kalikaluṣa-hatau</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;bhrātarau brahma-daityau</strong><br>
<strong>gāḍha-prema prakāśaiḥ kṛta-rudhira-vapuś</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;chāpi tāv-ujjahāra</strong><br>
<strong>śrī-nityānanda-chandraṁ bhaja bhaja satataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaura-kṛṣṇa-pradaṁ tam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Nityānanda Prabhu was ordered by the golden moon, Śrīman Mahāprabhu, to deliver the masses by the wide distribution of Lord Krishna’s Holy Names. One evening, Lord Nityānanda encountered those two drunken and sinful brothers, Jagāi and Mādhāi, who were previously <i>brahma-daitya</i> demons of the first order, acting under the devastating spell of <i>Kali-yuga</i>. Suddenly, Nitāi was attacked by Mādhāi, whereupon His body became smeared with blood. In response, Lord Nitāi exhibited deep affection for the welfare of the two brothers and rescued them from their abominable condition. Let us eternally worship that great personality Śrī Nityānandachandra, who freely grants the association of Śrī Gaurāṅgadev, who is none other than Lord Krishna.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="9">
<h5>9</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>sākṣād-gauro gaṇānāṁ śirasi yad</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;avadhūtasya kaupīna-khaṇḍaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>saṁdhartuñ chādideśāsava yavana vadhū</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;spṛṣṭa-dṛṣṭo ’pi vandyaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>brahmādyānām apīti prabhu-parihṛtakānām</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;api sveṣṭa-pīṭaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>śrī-nityānanda-chandraṁ bhaja bhaja satataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaura-kṛṣṇa-pradaṁ tam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Lord Gaurāṅga instructed His family members and followers to wear pieces of Lord Nityānanda’s kaupīna respectfully on their bodies. So pure and impeccable is the character of Nitāi that even if He is seen holding the hand of a Yavana’s wife and carrying a bottle of wine, nonetheless He remains supremely worshippable by the great demigods such as Lord Brahmā. Lord Nityānanda is also the desirable object of those exalted devotees who can steal away the heart of the Lord. Let us eternally worship that great personality Śrī Nityānanda-chandra, who freely grants the association of Śrī Gaurāṅgadev, who is none other than Lord Krishna.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="10">
<h5>10</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>uddhartuṁ jñāna-karmādy-apahata-charitān</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaura-chandro yad āsau</strong><br>
<strong>nyāsaṁ kṛtvā tu māyā mṛgamanusṛtavān</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;grāhayan kṛṣṇa-nāma</strong><br>
<strong>tach-chāyevānvadhāvat sthala-jala-gahane</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;yo ’pi tasyeṣṭa-cheṣṭaḥ</strong><br>
<strong>śrī nityānanda-chandraṁ bhaja bhaja satataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaura-kṛṣṇa-pradaṁ tam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Accepting the renounced order of life and distributing the Lord’s Holy Names, Lord Gaurāṅga chased after those souls who had been spoiled by the doctrines of dry argumentative logic and motivated fruitive activity. Like Lord Gaurāṅga’s own shadow, Nitāi Prabhu accompanied the Lord over land and through rivers, waterways, and jungles. In addition, Lord Nityānanda is the desired goal of all Gaurāṅga’s endeavours. Let us eternally worship that grand personality Śrī Nityānanda-chandra, who can freely grant the association of that golden Lord Śrī Gaurāṅgadev, who is none other than Lord Krishna.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="11">
<h5>11</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-rādhā-prema-lubdho divasa-niśi-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;tadāsvāda-mattaika līlo</strong><br>
<strong>gauro yañ-chādideśa svaparikara-vṛtaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;kṛṣṇa-nāma pradātum</strong><br>
<strong>gauḍe ’bādhaṁ dadau yaḥ subhaga-gaṇa-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;dhanaṁ gaura-nāma-prakāmaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>śrī-nityānanda-chandraṁ bhaja bhaja satataṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaura-kṛṣṇa-pradaṁ tam</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Day  and  night  Lord  Chaitanya  was intoxicated with the intense desire to taste the sweetness of Śrī Rādhikā’s divine love for Krishna. Śrīman Mahāprabhu  instructed  Lord  Nityānanda  and  His associates to go out and profusely distribute the Holy Names of Lord Krishna. However, Lord Nityānanda instead plentifully distributed Lord Gaurāṅga’s Name, the most precious fortune available to the living entities, with no impediment at all. Let us worship that great personality Śrī Nityānanda-chandra, who freely grants the association of Śrī Gaurāṅgadev, who is none other than Lord Krishna.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="12">
<h5>12</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>śrī-rādhā-kṛṣṇa-līlā-rasa-madhura-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;sudhāsvāda-śuddhaika-mūrtau</strong><br>
<strong>gaure śraddhāṁ dṛḍhāṁ bho prabhu-parikara-</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;samrāṭ prayacchādhame ’smin</strong><br>
<strong>ullaṅghyāṅghriṁ hi yasyākhila-bhajana-kathā</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;svapnavach chaiva mithyā</strong><br>
<strong>śrī-nityānanda-chandraṁ patita-śaraṇa-daṁ</strong><br>
<strong>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;gaura-daṁ taṁ bhaje ’ham</strong></p>
<p class="translation">O Nityānanda Prabhu, emperor amidst the personal associates of Śrīman Mahāprabhu! Kindly grant this fallen soul deep and resolute faith in that pure and divine figure of Śrī Gaurāṅga. Lord Gaurāṅga’s personality exclusively embodies the sweet nectar relished deep within the devotional mellows of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Krishna’s Pastimes. But if Your lotus feet are over-looked, than all so-called devotional orations and prayers meant for the highest worship become false, like a dream. I worship You, that great personality Nityānanda-chandra, who openly bestows shelter to the most fallen souls, freely granting the association of Śrī Gaurāṅgadev.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section class="song" id="song-guru-rupa-virajita-nanda-sutam" data-translated="1">
<div class="verse-block" data-number="1">
<h2>Śrī Gaurahari-Kusuma-Stavāṣṭakam <span class="nav-links">&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#toc">↑</a>&nbsp;&nbsp;<a href="#first-lines">↓</a></span></h2>
<h3>Eight Flower Offerings of Praise to Lord Gaurhari</h3>
<h4>Composed by Śrīla Bhakti Rakṣak Śrīdhar Dev-Goswāmī Mahārāj</h4>
<h5>1</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>guru-rūpa-virājita-nanda-sutaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>sutanūnnata-kāñchana-bhūmi-dharam</strong><br>
<strong>dharaṇī-jana-tāraṇa-tuṅga-tariṁ</strong><br>
<strong>bhaja gaurahariṁ bhaja gauraharim</strong></p>
<p class="translation">The son of Mahārāj Nanda, Svayaṁ-Bhagavān Śrī Krishna, is now illustrious in His divine form of Śrī Guru. He is beautiful and highly exalted, bearing His golden Persona. (Or, He is beautiful and highly exalted within the golden land of His divine abode.) He is the eminent lifeboat that rescues the souls of this world. Serve that Lord, Gaurahari, worship Gaurahari.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="2">
<h5>2</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>vṛṣabhānu-sutā-dyuti-bhāva-vṛtaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>vṛta-chitta-nijādbhuta-rūpa-madhum</strong><br>
<strong>madhu-vibhrama-nāmada-śaśva-dariṁ</strong><br>
<strong>bhaja gaurahariṁ bhaja gauraharim</strong></p>
<p class="translation">Adorned  with  the  heart  and  halo  of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī (Vṛṣabhānu-sutā), having dived deeply into the reality of His own wonderful beauty and sweetness, He is sweetly intoxicated in the perpetual distribution of His own Holy Name. Serve that Lord, Gaurahari, worship Gaurahari.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="3">
<h5>3</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>uditākhila-gaurava-gauḍapuraṁ</strong><br>
<strong>puraṭojjvala-maṅgala-mūrta-rasam</strong><br>
<strong>rasanotsava-kīrtana-kṛṣṇa-hariṁ</strong><br>
<strong>bhaja gaurahariṁ bhaja gauraharim</strong></p>
<p class="translation">He manifested the full glory of Śrī Nabadwip Dhām, as He appeared in His radiant gold, all-auspicious form of divine ecstasy, ever engaged in Krishna-<i>kīrtan</i>, which is a festival for the tongue. Serve that Lord, Gaurahari, worship Gaurahari.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="4">
<h5>4</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>nija-pārṣada-darśana-datta-śubhaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>śubha-nāma-sudhā-kṛta-matta-janam</strong><br>
<strong>janatāgha-hara-sthita-chitta-darim</strong><br>
<strong>bhaja gaurahariṁ bhaja gauraharim</strong></p>
<p class="translation">He gave the spiritually auspicious <i>darśan</i> of His eternal associates to the world (or, He gave His spiritually auspicious darśan to His eternal associates), He drove the masses of people mad with the nectar of His spiritually all-auspicious Name, vanquishing all sins from the core of their hearts. Serve that Lord, Gaurahari, worship Gaurahari.</p>
</div>
<div class="verse-block" data-number="5">
<h5>5</h5>
<p class="verse"><strong>dadataṁ satataṁ nija-nāma-dhanaṁ</strong><br>
<strong>dhana-māna na vai vanitāṁ kavitām</strong><br>
<strong>vitaran vraja-bhaktim-adatta-charīṁ</strong><br>
<strong>bhaja gaurahariṁ bhaja gauraharim</strong></p>
<p class="translation">He incessantly distributed the spiritual fortune of His own divine Name. He did not regard as fruitful the paths of endeavouring for material wealth, lusty material desires or grand material dreams as captured by poetry.
